Optional strata must be recorded **before final scoring**: for example, origins with absolute five-minute pump-flow change above the training 95th percentile of nonzero changes, or recent past 15-minute n1 change above a training quantile. Choose/validate the rule using 2018 only and freeze thresholds. Handle absent nonzero changes by omitting that stratum, not redefining it on 2019. If actual **future** pressure change is used for a retrospective error breakdown, label it an oracle/retrospective group; it cannot define a real-time feature or route a prediction. Do not use leak labels or future regimes for tuning.

## Four-week scope and limits

Week 1: student review, freeze preparation/split/metrics, build aligned rows. Week 2: E0 and two small regression families with limited validation settings. Week 3: matched E1-E4 comparisons; optional strata only if core work is complete. Week 4: final protocol freeze, one final assessment, reproducibility checks and joint interpretation. These are proposed tasks, not assigned members or completed work.

One simulated network and two calendar years cannot establish field generalization or worldwide novelty. Regime changes, seasonal shifts, model dependence and previously visible 2019 context limit conclusions. The plan seeks a useful, falsifiable course-project result, including the possibility that additional measurements do not help.
# Ahmed et al. (2025): shared measurements, different learning task

Post-feedback source reading, 2026-10-28. AI-assisted comparison pending student review; published results were not reproduced. Source: Ahmed, DÃ¼ÅŸtegÃ¶r, Lazovik and WÃ¶rtche, *Windowed Multivariate LSTM Autoencoder for Leak Detection in Water Distribution Networks*, CCWI 2025, [DOI and original record](https://doi.org/10.15131/shef.data.29921204.v1), [local licensed six-page PDF](../literature/papers/Ahmed_2025_Windowed_LSTM_Autoencoder.pdf). Page numbers below are PDF pages.

| Aspect | What the paper actually reports | Relation to this proposal |
|---|---|---|
| Data and signals, p. 2 | BattLeDIM DMA C, 2018/2019; n1, n4, n31, T1, PUMP_1. | These five signals were already combined in prior work. They cannot be claimed as a new five-signal selection. |
| Task, pp. 2-3 | Reconstruct multivariate input windows; elevated reconstruction error is used to detect leaks. Normal-operation training makes this a reconstruction/anomaly approach, not future-pressure supervised regression. | Our label is recorded n1 at t+15 min. AMR_n1 is a sixth candidate signal; its benefit remains untested. |
| Preparation, p. 3 | Moving z-score with a one-month window; overlapping windows of 288 five-minute samples (24 h). The two training segments are windowed separately before combination. | Use past-only lags, train-fitted preprocessing and explicit chronological boundaries; do not copy the paper's protocol as proof of causal deployment. |
| Models, pp. 3-4 / Fig. 1 | LSTM and 1D CNN autoencoders. Figure 1 shows LSTM encoded widths 64 then 32 and decoder widths 32 then 64, followed by reconstruction to 5x288. CNN uses pooling size 2 and dropout 0.2; the text specifies tanh in each model's first layer. Both minimize reconstruction MSE with Adam, learning rate 0.001, 10 epochs. | Course-scale regression is a simpler different task; no autoencoder implementation or accuracy replication is claimed here. |
| Detection, p. 4 | Binary segmentation on reconstruction errors, retaining positive changes for alarms; compared with PELT for change-point computation. | A time-to-detection result is not a +15-minute forecast horizon. |
| Metrics, pp. 2-5 | Normal reconstruction error; factor of mean reconstruction error (leak-state mean divided by normal-state mean); time to detection. | They are not forecast MAE/RMSE in metres and cannot be ranked against our persistence errors. |

## Split and leak treatment

Page 3 specifies training segments 2018-01-26 to 2018-06-28 and 2018-08-13 to 2019-01-14 (end times 23:55); test starts 2019-01-15. Date typography in the paper is normalized here. Persistent p257 is explicitly treated as part of normal baseline, while the mid-2018 p31 period is excluded. â€œNormalâ€ therefore does not mean leak-free. Fifteen percent of the training windows are randomly separated for validation.

Overlapping windows and random validation raise a risk of strong train/validation dependence; without author implementation details, this is a methodological caution rather than a proved data-leakage finding. The text does not establish whether moving z-score statistics are trailing or centered, nor an exact live-availability protocol. We do not infer those details. Page 5's phrase about no early-2019 leaks should be read alongside the explicit persistent-p257 baseline on p. 3; the paper does not support calling the full interval physically leak-free.

## Published results and their meaning

| Table / measure | CNN AE | LSTM AE | Meaning |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Table 1, p. 4: normal error | 0.0732 | 0.0031 | Reconstruction loss on standardized signals, not pressure error in metres |
| Table 1: one-leak MRE factor | 2.4034 | 9.7775 | Larger ratio indicates stronger separation from the normal baseline |
| Table 1: two-leak MRE factor | 4.2370 | 16.9816 | Same ratio for the multi-leak state |
| Table 2, p. 5: abrupt TTD | 21 h 25 min | 21 h 35 min | LSTM is slightly slower for this abrupt event |
| Table 2: incipient TTD | 117 h 20 min | 43 h 55 min | LSTM detects this gradual event sooner than the paper's own CNN |

Table 2 also lists a previous 1D CNN from another source (93 h 50 min for the incipient leak); it must not be confused with the paper's own CNN. These are author-reported results under their detection settings, not our measured performance.

This close prior work narrows the defensible contribution: test **incremental information for future n1**, including a controlled AMR_n1 addition, rather than claim the shared sensors are new. Ahmed is a separate post-feedback addition; the historical 47-source/35-group chart remains frozen and qualified as a curated inventory. See [screening boundaries](../literature/SEARCH_AND_SCREENING.md).
# Why Area C and n1: facts, rationale and limits

Post-feedback addition, 2026-10-28. AI-assisted draft pending student review. The supplied rationale expresses reasons for a manageable study; it does not document an independent student model audit.

## Sector comparison

The [official nominal INP and configuration](../data/source_manifest.json) give the following [reproduced pipe-component counts](../results/reproduced/topology_audit.json). Pumps and valves are excluded when defining these components; the two upstream boundary junctions are outside A/B/C.

| Area | Junctions | Pressure channels | AMR channels | Boundary and proposed selection reasoning |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| A | 657 | 29 | 0 | PRV-1/2 feed the main network. More peer pressure channels offer opportunities, but a broader candidate set than needed for this project. No evidence that A is harder to forecast. |
| B | 31 | 1: n215 | 0 | PRV-3 feeds the regulated component. One monitored pressure node limits within-sector peer-sensor comparisons. A regulated inlet does not make remote pressure constant. |
| C | 92 | 3: n1, n4, n31 | 82 | Tank-fed; PUMP_1 connects n54 in A to T1. Three pressure channels, tank level and measured inflow support a small interpretable comparison. Select AMR_n1 from the 82 available local demand channels. |

Area A has the most **pressure channels**, not the most measurement channels overall. C plus pump inflow supplies 87 candidate channels (82+3+1+1), reduced to six chosen signal series. This is an engineering filter, not demonstrated feature importance. A tank supplies storage buffering, but it does not prove upstream statistical independence: pump operation still links upstream conditions and tank state.

## Target geometry verified against both official models

New [static audit output](../results/engineering/model_geometry_verification.json), supported by [source/code hashes](../results/engineering/audit_manifest.json):

| Junction | Model elevation (m), nominal and generator | Nominal shortest pipe path from T1 (m) | Generator shortest pipe path from T1 (m) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| n1 | 73.2105 | 891.3208 | 881.9323 |
| n4 | 68.2608 | 573.7565 | 574.9663 |
| n31 | 65.0059 | 307.7926 | 306.7286 |
| n22, unmonitored by a pressure sensor | 76.1173 | Not used for target selection | Not used for target selection |

n1 is highest and has the longest T1 shortest pipe path among the three monitored C pressure nodes. It is **not** highest in C: n22 is, and n1 ranks 24th among the 92 C junctions in both models. Elevations are in model metres; the INP does not certify a sea-level datum. These facts motivate a remote, relatively high-elevation **monitored target**, not a proven minimum-pressure or globally critical node. Pressure also depends on head loss, demand and operating state.

The generator checksum matches [Zenodo's exact file](https://zenodo.org/api/records/4017659/files/L-TOWN_Real.inp/content). Selected elevations agree, but pipe paths differ; nominal tank diameter is 16.0000 m and generator diameter is 15.4251 m. Do not substitute one geometry for the other or force agreement. No pressure simulation or sensitivity calculation was performed.

Both static INPs list PRV settings of 40/50/35 m and pump controls at T1 above 3.9 m / below 2.4 m. This verifies model entries, not measured valve-status histories or constant remote pressures. Nominal shortest pipe lengths are not transport delays, hydraulic sensitivities or evidence of sensor value.

Reproduce the optional generator check after the ordinary data download:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts/audit_engineering_rationale.py --download-generator
```

This downloads an additional 177 MB into ignored `data/raw/`. A verified cache can instead be passed with `--generator path/to/L-TOWN_Real.inp`. Only static sections are retained; no forecast is evaluated. [Student questions](STUDENT_REVIEW_LOG.md) distinguish agreement with this rationale from actual personal execution.
